# AWA — Medical translation validation checklist (12 African languages)

Status of this document: **prepared for human review. No native-speaker or clinical approval exists.** Every "Reviewer decision" is `Pending` and every comment cell is empty on purpose. Nothing in this file has been validated by a person; the translations themselves were written and reviewed by AI only (see `AFRICAN_LANGUAGES_MEDICAL_REVIEW.md`).

Prepared: 2026-11-02. Source report: `docs/localization/AFRICAN_LANGUAGES_MEDICAL_REVIEW.md`. Translations were **not** modified when this file was produced.

## How to use

1. Each reviewer takes the section of their language (sections 3.1 to 3.12). Fulfulde reviewers must first read section 4; every reviewer should check section 5 (pregnancy-week food names).
2. For every row, read the English source and the current translation. Set **Reviewer decision** to `Approved`, `Correction required` or `Pending` (leave `Pending` if you did not review the row). If a correction is required, write the proposed text in **Reviewer comments**.
3. A row groups all dictionary keys whose current text is identical. A correction made once should be applied to every listed key (the first four keys are shown; the file path is in the section heading).
4. Medical and religious rows (Critical / High) should be signed by a health professional or, for Nifas and Istihada, a qualified religious reviewer, in addition to the linguist. Record the name and date in the sign-off table at the end of each section.
5. Do not translate placeholders such as `{{count}}`, `{{date}}`, `{{name}}`.

## 1. Priority order

1. Pregnancy loss / miscarriage (Critical to High: tone and meaning)
2. Cervical mucus
3. Fertile window and ovulation
4. Menstruation / period
5. Postpartum bleeding and lochia
6. Nifas and Istihada (religious validation required)
7. Hot flashes, hormonal acne, contraception terms, breastfeeding, partner
8. Article-level findings, Fulfulde dialect (section 4), food names (section 5)

## 2. Severity scale

| Severity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Critical | Could change the meaning of a medical or sensitive statement (for example implying an induced abortion) |
| High | A wrong or unverified term for a concept the user relies on for health decisions, or a religious term |
| Medium | Inconsistent or unverified term with limited risk |
| Low | Cosmetic or low-risk wording |

## 4. Fulfulde (`ff`) — dialect decision

**Decision required before any further review of `ff`.** Do not review individual rows until this is decided, because verbs and nouns would have to be rewritten.

Evidence in `mobile/src/i18n/locales/ff.ts` and the ff blocks of the articles:

| Feature | Observed in the text | Points to |
|---|---|---|
| Infinitives and verbal nouns | -ugo / -ugol (nonnugo, danyugo, ndaarugo, Hunnugol, hoosugo) | Maasina / Adamawa-type Fulfulde (Mali, Nigeria, Cameroon) |
| Pulaar pattern | -ude / -ɗe forms are the Senegalese / Guinean norm and are mostly absent | — |
| Pregnancy | reedu, Ɓesngu, Ɓernde (three words) | mixed |
| Period | Mensturasiyoŋ, mense, Regle, haila, lewru, Menstruasion | French loanwords beside Fulfulde and Hausa-influenced words |
| Birth | danyugo, danyeere | mixed |
| App locale and flag | `ff-Latn-SN`, Senegal flag | Pulaar (Senegal) |

Options:

| Option | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| A. Pulaar (Senegal / Guinea) | Matches the flag, the locale and the francophone audience; French medical loanwords already used | Most of the current verb forms would need rewriting |
| B. Fulfulde Maasina / Adamawa (Mali, Nigeria, Cameroon) | Closest to the current morphology; larger number of speakers | Locale and flag would change; less francophone |
| C. Keep a neutral loanword-heavy register | Least rewriting | Still mixed; reviewers will disagree on every row |

Decision to record:

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Chosen variety | Pending |
| Decided by (name, role) | |
| Date | |
| Reviewer assigned for that variety | |
| Notes | |

## 6. Counts and urgent risks

| Language | Code | Terms awaiting validation (rows) | Critical | High | Food names |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kabyle | kab | 112 | 0 | 79 | 0 |
| Oromo | om | 112 | 0 | 78 | 13 |
| Tigrinya | ti | 107 | 0 | 79 | 0 |
| Kinyarwanda | rw | 103 | 1 | 76 | 3 |
| Kirundi | rn | 105 | 1 | 76 | 7 |
| Igbo | ig | 110 | 0 | 77 | 20 |
| Yoruba | yo | 99 | 0 | 72 | 12 |
| Zulu | zu | 106 | 0 | 80 | 0 |
| Xhosa | xh | 111 | 0 | 79 | 0 |
| Shona | sn | 105 | 0 | 74 | 17 |
| Lingala | ln | 104 | 0 | 77 | 1 |
| Fulfulde / Fula | ff | 114 | 0 | 79 | 1 |
| **Total** | | **1288** | | | |

Most urgent medical risks (in order):

1. **Pregnancy loss wording** in Kinyarwanda and Kirundi ("gukuramo inda", commonly induced abortion), Yoruba ("ìṣẹ́yún"), Lingala ("kobungisa"): a bereaved user could read an accusation or an abortion.
2. **Cervical mucus** in every language (3–5 renderings each; zu and xh previously had "redness"; yo is a loanword; xh uses two different terms): misreadings change what the user records for fertility.
3. **Fertile window** (zu "sokuvundisa" = ferment; ln "kobota" = give birth) and ovulation: used to plan or avoid pregnancy.
4. **Nifas, Istihada, lochia**: transliterated without explanation; religious rulings differ by school and must be reviewed by a qualified person; lochia must be recognisable next to warning signs.
5. **Menstruation terms** that mean "time" or "month" (zu, ln, sn) and mixed spellings (ti, ff).
6. **Fulfulde dialect**: no row of ff can be approved until section 4 is decided.
